Proof

We treat the cases \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha } \in (0, +\infty )$$\end{document}α¯∈(0,+∞), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha } = 0$$\end{document}α¯=0, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }= +\infty $$\end{document}α¯=+∞ separately.

Let us first assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha } \in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯∈(0,+∞). The proof of this case essentially follows the computations in [47, Thm. 2.4.20], but since our notation and focus are different, we present a complete proof adapted to our setting. Being \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}uαj a minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_{\alpha _j}[u]$$\end{document}Fαj[u] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}uα¯ a minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_{\bar{\alpha }}[u]$$\end{document}Fα¯[u], we get that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} u_\eta - u_{\bar{\alpha }}&= \bar{\alpha }p_{\bar{\alpha }} \quad \text { with } p_{\bar{\alpha }} \in \partial TV[u_{\bar{\alpha }}],\\ u_\eta - u_{\alpha _j}&= \alpha _j p_{\alpha _j} \quad \text { with } p_{\alpha _j} \in \partial TV[u_{\alpha _j}], \end{aligned}$$\end{document}uη-uα¯=α¯pα¯withpα¯∈∂TV[uα¯],uη-uαj=αjpαjwithpαj∈∂TV[uαj],where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial TV$$\end{document}∂TV denotes the subdifferential in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω) of TV (extended to be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$+\infty $$\end{document}+∞ on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega ){\setminus } BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω)\BV(Ω)). Multiplying the first equality by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j / \bar{\alpha }$$\end{document}αj/α¯ and subtracting the second one from it, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \alpha _j (p_{\bar{\alpha }} - p_{\alpha _j})&= \frac{\alpha _j}{\bar{\alpha }} (u_\eta - u_{\bar{\alpha }}) - (u_\eta - u_{\alpha _j})\\&=\left( \frac{\alpha _j}{\bar{\alpha }}-1\right) (u_\eta - u_{\bar{\alpha }}) + u_{\alpha _j} - u_{\bar{\alpha }}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}αj(pα¯-pαj)=αjα¯(uη-uα¯)-(uη-uαj)=αjα¯-1(uη-uα¯)+uαj-uα¯.Multiplying the preceding identity by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }} - u_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}uα¯-uαj, integrating over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω, and using the monotonicity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial TV$$\end{document}∂TV, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0 \leqslant \left( \frac{\alpha _j}{\bar{\alpha }}-1\right) \int _\Omega (u_\eta - u_{\bar{\alpha }})(u_{\bar{\alpha }} {-} u_{\alpha _j})\;\text {d}x {-} \Vert u_{\bar{\alpha }} {-} u_{\alpha _j}\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}0⩽αjα¯-1∫Ω(uη-uα¯)(uα¯-uαj)dx-‖uα¯-uαj‖L2(Ω)2.Consequently, using Cauchy–Schwarz’s inequality, and reorganizing the terms, it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u_{\bar{\alpha }} - u_{\alpha _j}\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )} \leqslant \frac{|\alpha _j - \bar{\alpha }|}{\bar{\alpha }} \Vert u_\eta - u_{\bar{\alpha }}\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uα¯-uαj‖L2(Ω)⩽|αj-α¯|α¯‖uη-uα¯‖L2(Ω).On the other hand, taking into account that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }} = {\text {argmin}}_{L^2(\Omega )} F_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}uα¯=argminL2(Ω)Fα¯, we have that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u_{\bar{\alpha }} - u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}&\leqslant \Vert u_{\bar{\alpha }} - u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} + \bar{\alpha } TV(u_{\bar{\alpha }},\Omega )\\&=F_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}] \leqslant F_{\bar{\alpha }}[0] = \Vert u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uα¯-uη‖L2(Ω)2⩽‖uα¯-uη‖L2(Ω)2+α¯TV(uα¯,Ω)=Fα¯[uα¯]⩽Fα¯[0]=‖uη‖L2(Ω)2,which, together with the preceding estimate, yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u_{\bar{\alpha }} - u_{\alpha _j}\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )} \leqslant \frac{|\alpha _j - \bar{\alpha }|}{\bar{\alpha }} \Vert u_\eta \Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uα¯-uαj‖L2(Ω)⩽|αj-α¯|α¯‖uη‖L2(Ω).We now consider the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=0$$\end{document}α¯=0 case. Because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω is a bounded, Lipschitz domain, we can find a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\hat{u}_\kappa )_{\kappa \in \mathbb {N}}\in C^\infty (\overline{\Omega })\subset BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}(u^κ)κ∈N∈C∞(Ω¯)⊂BV(Ω) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{u}_\kappa \rightarrow u_\eta $$\end{document}u^κ→uη in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω). Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}(αj)-12→∞, we can modify \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\hat{u}_\kappa )_{\kappa \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(u^κ)κ∈N by repeating each of its elements as (finitely) many times as necessary so that the resulting sequence, denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uj)j∈N, satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TV(u_j,\Omega )\leqslant (\alpha _j)^{-\frac{1}{2}}$$\end{document}TV(uj,Ω)⩽(αj)-12 for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}j∈N large enough. Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_j\rightarrow u_\eta $$\end{document}uj→uη in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \lim _{j\rightarrow \infty }\alpha _jTV(u_j,\Omega )=0$$\end{document}limj→∞αjTV(uj,Ω)=0. Using this sequence in the minimality of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}uαj results in\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u_{\alpha _j}- & u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} + \alpha _j TV(u_{\alpha _j}) \\ & \quad \leqslant \Vert u_{j} - u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} + \alpha _j TV(u_{j}), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uαj-uη‖L2(Ω)2+αjTV(uαj)⩽‖uj-uη‖L2(Ω)2+αjTV(uj),Because both terms on the right-hand side converge to zero, we conclude that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_{\alpha _j})_{j \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uαj)j∈N converges to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_\eta $$\end{document}uη strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω), as well.

We are left to treat the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }= +\infty $$\end{document}α¯=+∞ case. First, we claim that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[u_{\alpha _j}]_\Omega = [u_{\eta }]_\Omega $$\end{document}[uαj]Ω=[uη]Ω for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}j∈N. To see this, we use \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j} = {\text {argmin}}_{u \in BV(\Omega )} F_{\alpha _j}[u]$$\end{document}uαj=argminu∈BV(Ω)Fαj[u] to get for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}c∈R that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} + \alpha _j TV(u_{\alpha _j},\Omega )\\ & \quad \leqslant \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta - c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} + \alpha _j TV(u_{\alpha _j},\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uαj-uη‖L2(Ω)2+αjTV(uαj,Ω)⩽‖uαj-uη-c‖L2(Ω)2+αjTV(uαj,Ω).Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} \leqslant \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta - c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} $$\end{document}‖uαj-uη‖L2(Ω)2⩽‖uαj-uη-c‖L2(Ω)2. Moreover, we also know that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} [u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta ]_\Omega = {\text {argmin}}_{c \in \mathbb {R}} \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta - c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}[uαj-uη]Ω=argminc∈R‖uαj-uη-c‖L2(Ω)2with only one minimizer by strict convexity, which would lead to a contradiction with the previous inequality unless \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta ]_\Omega = 0$$\end{document}[uαj-uη]Ω=0. In other words, we must have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[u_{\alpha _j}] = [u_\eta ]_\Omega $$\end{document}[uαj]=[uη]Ω for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}j∈N. To conclude, we use the estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}] \leqslant \Vert u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}$$\end{document}Fαj[uαj]⩽‖uη‖L2(Ω)2 as above, which by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$F_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}Fαj implies that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{j \rightarrow \infty } TV(u_{\alpha _j},\Omega ) = 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limj→∞TV(uαj,Ω)=0.Moreover, by the Poincaré inequality, we have that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - [u_\eta ]_{\Omega }\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )} & = \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - [u_{\alpha _j}]_{\Omega }\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}\\ & \leqslant C \, TV(u_{\alpha _j},\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uαj-[uη]Ω‖L2(Ω)=‖uαj-[uαj]Ω‖L2(Ω)⩽CTV(uαj,Ω).Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_{\alpha _j})_{j \in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uαj)j∈N converges to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[u_\eta ]_{\Omega }$$\end{document}[uη]Ω strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

From the preceding lemma, we immediately deduce the following corollary.

Corollary 3.11

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}Ω⊂R2 be a bounded, Lipschitz domain, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I:(0,+\infty )\rightarrow [0,+\infty )$$\end{document}I:(0,+∞)→[0,+∞) be the function defined in (3.11). Then, I can be extended continuously to a function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{I}:[0,+\infty ]\rightarrow [0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}I^:[0,+∞]→[0,+∞] defined for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }\in [0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}α¯∈[0,+∞] by3.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \widehat{I} (\bar{\alpha }) = {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} I(\alpha )=\Vert u_\alpha - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} & \text {if } \bar{\alpha }= \alpha \in (0,+\infty ),\\ \Vert u_\eta - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} & \text {if } \bar{\alpha }=0,\\ \Vert [ u_\eta ]_\Omega - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} & \text {if } \bar{\alpha }=+\infty . \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}I^(α¯)=I(α)=‖uα-uc‖L2(Ω)2ifα¯=α∈(0,+∞),‖uη-uc‖L2(Ω)2ifα¯=0,‖[uη]Ω-uc‖L2(Ω)2ifα¯=+∞.

Remark 3.12

We observe that the only continuity condition on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{I}$$\end{document}I^ needed for our analysis to hold is that of lower semicontinuity of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{I}$$\end{document}I^, as given by (2.6). However, because it is not hard to prove continuity on the whole of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0, +\infty ]$$\end{document}[0,+∞] in the TV case, we have done so in the results above, which we believe to be of interest on their own.

Proof of Theorem 3.8

We will proceed in three steps.

Step 1. \beta )TV(u_\beta ,\Omega ) \\&\quad {\leqslant }\Vert u_\alpha -u_\eta \Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega ) } {+} \beta TV(u_\alpha ,\Omega ) + (\alpha - \beta )TV(u_\beta ,\Omega ), \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uα-uη‖L2(Ω)2+αTV(uα,Ω)⩽‖uβ-uη‖L2(Ω)2+αTV(uβ,Ω)=‖uβ-uη‖L2(Ω)2+βTV(uβ,Ω)+(α-β)TV(uβ,Ω)⩽‖uα-uη‖L2(Ω)2+βTV(uα,Ω)+(α-β)TV(uβ,Ω),from which we get that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\beta TV(u_\beta ,\Omega ) \leqslant \beta TV(u_\eta ,\Omega ) \\&\quad \quad \text {and} \\ &(\alpha - \beta ) TV(u_\alpha ,\Omega ) \leqslant (\alpha - \beta )TV(u_\beta ,\Omega ). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}βTV(uβ,Ω)⩽βTV(uη,Ω)and(α-β)TV(uα,Ω)⩽(α-β)TV(uβ,Ω).Hence, recalling that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta >0$$\end{document}β>0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha -\beta <0$$\end{document}α-β<0, it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TV(u_\beta ,\Omega )\leqslant TV(u_\eta ,\Omega )$$\end{document}TV(uβ,Ω)⩽TV(uη,Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ TV(u_\alpha ,\Omega ) \geqslant TV(u_\beta ,\Omega )$$\end{document}TV(uα,Ω)⩾TV(uβ,Ω). Finally, using the first of these estimates and Lemma 3.10 with an arbitrary decreasing sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\beta _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(βj)j∈N converging to 0, the lower-semicontinuity of the total variation with respect to the strong convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1$$\end{document}L1 yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} TV(u_\eta ,\Omega )&\geqslant \limsup _{j\rightarrow \infty } TV(u_{\beta _j},\Omega )\\&\geqslant \liminf _{j\rightarrow \infty } TV(u_{\beta _j},\Omega )\geqslant TV(u_\eta ,\Omega ). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TV(uη,Ω)⩾lim supj→∞TV(uβj,Ω)⩾lim infj→∞TV(uβj,Ω)⩾TV(uη,Ω).This concludes the proof of (3.17).

Step 2. We prove that if condition ii) in the statement holds, (i.e., \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert u_\eta - u_c\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}\\&\quad \leqslant \limsup _{j\rightarrow \infty }\Big ( \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_\eta \Vert _{L^2(\Omega )} +\Vert u_\eta - u_c\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )} \Big ) \\&\quad = \Vert u_\eta - u_c\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )} <\Vert [ u_\eta ]_\Omega - u_c\Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}, \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→∞‖uαj-uc‖L2(Ω)⩽lim supj→∞(‖uαj-uη‖L2(Ω)+‖uη-uc‖L2(Ω))=‖uη-uc‖L2(Ω)<‖[uη]Ω-uc‖L2(Ω),from which (3.18) follows.

Step 3. We conclude the proof of Theorem 3.8.

We first show (3.11). Because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{I} $$\end{document}I^ is a lower- semicontinuous function on the compact set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}[0,+∞], \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{I} $$\end{document}I^ attains a minimum on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}[0,+∞]. By (3.13), (3.14), and (3.18), we conclude that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{I} $$\end{document}I^ attains its minimum at some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha ^*\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α∗∈(0,+∞). Thus, using (3.13) once more,3.19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} I(\alpha ^*)&=\widehat{I} (\alpha ^*)=\min _{\bar{\alpha } \in [0,+\infty ]} \widehat{I} (\bar{\alpha })\\&=\min _{\alpha \in (0,+\infty )} \widehat{I} (\alpha )=\min _{\alpha \in (0,+\infty )} I(\alpha ), \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}I(α∗)=I^(α∗)=minα¯∈[0,+∞]I^(α¯)=minα∈(0,+∞)I^(α)=minα∈(0,+∞)I(α),which yields (3.11).

Next, to prove the existence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_\Omega $$\end{document}cΩ as stated, assume that there exist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha ^*_j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}(αj∗)j∈N⊂(0,+∞) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha ^*_j\rightarrow 0$$\end{document}αj∗→0 and (3.19) holds with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha ^*=\alpha ^*_j$$\end{document}α∗=αj∗. Next, we prove Theorem 1.4.

Proof of Theorem 1.4

In view of Theorem 3.6 (also see Remark 3.7), the statement in (a) follows. We conclude this section with some examples of stopping criteria for the refinement of the admissible partitions as defined in Definition 1.2.

Example 3.13

Here, we give an example of a stopping criterion that, heuristically, means that we only refine a given dyadic square \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L$$\end{document}L, if the distance of the restored image in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L$$\end{document}L to the clean image is greater than or equal to the sum of the distances of the restored images in each of the subdivisions of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L$$\end{document}L to the clean image, modulo a threshold that is determined by the user.

To make this idea precise, we introduce some notation. \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ L^{(2)}$$\end{document}L(2), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^{(3)}$$\end{document}L(3), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} Using this notation, and setting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_L=u_{\alpha _L}$$\end{document}uL=uαL (see (1.5)), we fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta >0$$\end{document}δ>0 and set up an admissible criteria as follows:3.20As we prove next,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \bar{\mathscr {P}}:=\big \{\mathscr {L}\in \mathscr {P}\!:\, L \text { satisfies }(\mathscr {S}) \text {for all }L\in \mathscr {L}\big \} \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}P¯:={L∈P:Lsatisfies(S)for allL∈L}has finite cardinality, which shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathscr {S})$$\end{document}(S) as above provides a stopping criteria for the refinement of the admissible partition.

Analysis of the Regularized Weighted-TV and Weighted-Fidelity Learning Schemes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV\!}_{\omega _\epsilon }}$$\end{document}(LS)TVωϵ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV-Fid}_\omega }$$\end{document}(LS)TV-Fidω

The results proved in the preceding section for the weighted-TV learning scheme can be easily adapted to the case of the regularized weighted-TV and the weighted-fidelity learning schemes, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV\!}_{\omega _\epsilon }}$$\end{document}(LS)TVωϵ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV-Fid}_\omega }$$\end{document}(LS)TV-Fidω. For the former, we prove here only Proposition 1.6 and provide an example of a sequence of regularized weights satisfying the conditions assumed in this result. Moreover, we highlight a question that is intimately related to the convergence of the solutions to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV\!}_{\omega _\epsilon }}$$\end{document}(LS)TVωϵ as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\epsilon \rightarrow 0^+$$\end{document}ϵ→0+ (see Subsect. 4.1). Regarding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV-Fid}_\omega }$$\end{document}(LS)TV-Fidω, and for completeness, we state the analogue existence and equivalence results for the weighted-fidelity learning scheme (see Subsect. 4.2).

The \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV\!}_{\omega _\epsilon }}$$\end{document}(LS)TVωϵ Learning Scheme

Next, we prove Proposition 1.6 and provide an example of a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\omega _\mathscr {L}^\epsilon )_\epsilon $$\end{document}(ωLϵ)ϵ as in (1.14).

Proof of Proposition 1.6

We show that4.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} E_\mathscr {L}^\epsilon [u] \leqslant E_\mathscr {L}[u] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}ELϵ[u]⩽EL[u]for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^1(Q)$$\end{document}u∈L1(Q), from which (1.15) follows.

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^1(Q)$$\end{document}u∈L1(Q) be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E_\mathscr {L}[u]< \infty $$\end{document}EL[u]<∞. Then, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_{\omega _\mathscr {L}}(Q)$$\end{document}u∈BVωL(Q) and recalling the definition and properties of the space of weighted \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV$$\end{document}BV-function discussed in Sect. 3.2, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_{\omega _\mathscr {L}^\epsilon }(Q)$$\end{document}u∈BVωLϵ(Q) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TV_{\omega _\mathscr {L}^\epsilon }(u,Q) \leqslant TV_{\omega _\mathscr {L}}(u,Q)$$\end{document}TVωLϵ(u,Q)⩽TVωL(u,Q), using the estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _\mathscr {L}^\epsilon \leqslant {\omega _\mathscr {L}}$$\end{document}ωLϵ⩽ωL a.e. in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Q$$\end{document}Q in (1.14). Thus, (4.1) holds. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Theorem 1.5).

Remark 4.3

An interesting question is whether condition (1.14) yields the convergence4.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \lim _{\epsilon \rightarrow 0^+} TV_{\omega _\mathscr {L}^\epsilon }(u,Q) = TV_{\omega _\mathscr {L}}(u,Q) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limϵ→0+TVωLϵ(u,Q)=TVωL(u,Q)for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_{\omega _\mathscr {L}}(Q)$$\end{document}u∈BVωL(Q). Because sets of zero Lebesgue measure may not have zero |Du| measure, we do not expect (4.10) to hold unless the almost everywhere pointwise convergence in (1.14) is replaced by everywhere pointwise convergence.

To the best of our knowledge, the closest result in this direction is [15, Lemma 2.1.4], which shows the following. Given a dyadic square \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\subset Q$$\end{document}L⊂Q and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha \in (0,\infty )$$\end{document}α∈(0,∞), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&{\text {argmin}}\left\{ \frac{1}{\alpha }\int _{L}|u_\eta -u|^2\;\text {d}x+ TV(u,L)\!:\,u\in BV(L)\right\} \\ &\qquad = {\text {argmin}}\left\{ \int _{L}|u_\eta -u|^2\;\text {d}x+ \alpha TV(u,L)\!:\,u\in BV(L)\right\} . \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}argmin1α∫L|uη-u|2dx+TV(u,L):u∈BV(L)=argmin∫L|uη-u|2dx+αTV(u,L):u∈BV(L).Consequently, Proposition 3.5 and Theorem 3.8 remain unchanged if we replace (1.6) by (1.18). These two results are the main tools to prove Theorems 1.4 and 1.5. Using this observation, the arguments used in Sect. 3 can be reproduced here for the weighted-fidelity learning scheme to conclude the two following theorems.

Remark 5.2

Recalling (2.4), we are using an abuse of notation in the preceding definition as we are not requiring \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du$$\end{document}Du nor \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}v$$\end{document}Ev to be Radon measures. However, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV(\Omega ) $$\end{document}u∈BV(Ω), then (5.1) is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω-weighted variation of the Radon measure \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du-v:= Du-v\mathcal {L}^n\lfloor \Omega \in \mathcal {M}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Du-v:=Du-vLn⌊Ω∈M(Ω;Rn) in the sense of (2.4). Similarly, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}v∈BD(Ω), then (5.2) is the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω-weighted variation of the Radon measure \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}v\in \mathcal {M}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^{n\times n}_{\text {s}ym})$$\end{document}Ev∈M(Ω;Rsymn×n) in the sense of (2.4).

Analogously to the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV\!}_\omega }$$\end{document}(LS)TVω case, we analyze each level of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TGV\!}_\omega }$$\end{document}(LS)TGVω in a dedicated subsection.

In this case, we replace (1.21) by5.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} Theorem 5.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn be an open set and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega :\Omega \rightarrow [0,\infty )$$\end{document}ω:Ω→[0,∞) a locally integrable function. Then, the following statements hold: (i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\inf _\Omega \omega >0$$\end{document}infΩω>0, then the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\mapsto \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )$$\end{document}v↦Vω(Ev,Ω) is lower-semicontinuous with respect to the (strong) convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn).(ii)Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )=\mathscr {V}_{\omega ^{sc^-}}(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )$$\end{document}Vω(Ev,Ω)=Vωsc-(Ev,Ω), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega ^{sc^-}$$\end{document}ωsc- denotes the lower-semicontinuous envelope of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω.(iii)Assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive. Then, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lloc1(Ω;Rn) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )<\infty $$\end{document}Vω(Ev,Ω)<∞ if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in BD_{\textrm{loc}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}v∈BDloc(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^1(\Omega ;\vert \mathcal {E}v\vert )$$\end{document}ω∈L1(Ω;|Ev|). If any of these two equivalent conditions hold, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,B)= \int _B \omega (x)\;\text {d}|\mathcal {E}v|(x) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Ev,B)=∫Bω(x)d|Ev|(x) for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω.(iv)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^\infty _{\text {loc}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}ω∈Lloc∞(Ω) is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive, then all bounded sequences in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD_{\omega }(\Omega )$$\end{document}BDω(Ω) are precompact in the strong \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}}$$\end{document}Lω,loc1-topology.

(i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\inf _\Omega \omega >0$$\end{document}infΩω>0, then the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\mapsto \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )$$\end{document}v↦Vω(Ev,Ω) is lower-semicontinuous with respect to the (strong) convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\inf _\Omega \omega >0$$\end{document}infΩω>0, then the map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\mapsto \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )$$\end{document}v↦Vω(Ev,Ω) is lower-semicontinuous with respect to the (strong) convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn).

(ii)Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )=\mathscr {V}_{\omega ^{sc^-}}(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )$$\end{document}Vω(Ev,Ω)=Vωsc-(Ev,Ω), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega ^{sc^-}$$\end{document}ωsc- denotes the lower-semicontinuous envelope of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω.

Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )=\mathscr {V}_{\omega ^{sc^-}}(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )$$\end{document}Vω(Ev,Ω)=Vωsc-(Ev,Ω), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega ^{sc^-}$$\end{document}ωsc- denotes the lower-semicontinuous envelope of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω.

(iii)Assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive. Then, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lloc1(Ω;Rn) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )<\infty $$\end{document}Vω(Ev,Ω)<∞ if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in BD_{\textrm{loc}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}v∈BDloc(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^1(\Omega ;\vert \mathcal {E}v\vert )$$\end{document}ω∈L1(Ω;|Ev|). If any of these two equivalent conditions hold, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,B)= \int _B \omega (x)\;\text {d}|\mathcal {E}v|(x) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Ev,B)=∫Bω(x)d|Ev|(x) for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω.

Assume \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive. Then, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lloc1(Ω;Rn) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,\Omega )<\infty $$\end{document}Vω(Ev,Ω)<∞ if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in BD_{\textrm{loc}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}v∈BDloc(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^1(\Omega ;\vert \mathcal {E}v\vert )$$\end{document}ω∈L1(Ω;|Ev|). If any of these two equivalent conditions hold, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,B)= \int _B \omega (x)\;\text {d}|\mathcal {E}v|(x) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Ev,B)=∫Bω(x)d|Ev|(x) for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω.

(iv)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^\infty _{\text {loc}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}ω∈Lloc∞(Ω) is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive, then all bounded sequences in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD_{\omega }(\Omega )$$\end{document}BDω(Ω) are precompact in the strong \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}}$$\end{document}Lω,loc1-topology.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^\infty _{\text {loc}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}ω∈Lloc∞(Ω) is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive, then all bounded sequences in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD_{\omega }(\Omega )$$\end{document}BDω(Ω) are precompact in the strong \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}}$$\end{document}Lω,loc1-topology.

Proof

Accounting for the fact that test functions here take values in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^{n\times n}_{\textrm{sym}}$$\end{document}Rsymn×n, the proof of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(i)$$\end{document}(i), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(ii)$$\end{document}(ii), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(iii)$$\end{document}(iii) may be obtained by mimicking that of [15, Proposition 1.3.1], [15, Proposition 2.1.1], and [15, Theorem 2.1.5], respectively.

To prove (iv), we observe that for each compact set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K\subset \Omega $$\end{document}K⊂Ω, there exists a positive constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_K$$\end{document}cK such that 0< \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tfrac{1}{c_K} \leqslant \omega \leqslant c_K$$\end{document}1cK⩽ω⩽cK in K because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^\infty _{\text {loc}}(\Omega )$$\end{document}ω∈Lloc∞(Ω) and strictly positive lower-semicontinuous functions are locally bounded away from zero. Then, using (iii), we have for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in BD_\omega (\Omega )$$\end{document}v∈BDω(Ω) that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(\mathcal {E}v,K)=\int _K \omega (x)\;\text {d}|\mathcal {E}v|(x) {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \geqslant \tfrac{1}{c_K} |\mathcal {E}v|(K),\\ \leqslant c_K |\mathcal {E}v|(K), \end{array}\right. }\\&\Vert v\Vert _{L^1_{\omega }(K;\mathbb {R}^n)}=\int _\Omega |v(x)|\,\omega (x)\;\text {d}x {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \geqslant \tfrac{1}{c_K} \Vert v\Vert _{L^1(K;\mathbb {R}^n)},\\ \leqslant c_K \Vert v\Vert _{L^1(K;\mathbb {R}^n)}. \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Ev,K)=∫Kω(x)d|Ev|(x)⩾1cK|Ev|(K),⩽cK|Ev|(K),‖v‖Lω1(K;Rn)=∫Ω|v(x)|ω(x)dx⩾1cK‖v‖L1(K;Rn),⩽cK‖v‖L1(K;Rn).The preceding estimates and the compact embedding of BD(K) into \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1(K;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}L1(K;Rn) (cf. [62]) yield \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(iv)$$\end{document}(iv). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Remark 5.4

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega :\Omega \rightarrow (0,\infty )$$\end{document}ω:Ω→(0,∞) is a lower-semicontinuous function satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<c\leqslant \inf _\Omega \omega \leqslant \sup _\Omega \omega \leqslant c^{-1}$$\end{document}0<c⩽infΩω⩽supΩω⩽c-1 for some positive constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c$$\end{document}c, then the arguments in the preceding proof show that Theorem 5.3 \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(iv)$$\end{document}(iv) holds globally in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω. In other words, bounded sequences in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD_{\omega }(\Omega )$$\end{document}BDω(Ω) are precompact in the strong \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega }(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lω1(Ω;Rn)-topology.

Remark 5.5

Differently from the weighted-TV case (cf. Theorem 3.1), we need the weights \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω in Theorem 5.3 to be bounded from below away from zero for item (i) to hold. This is because one cannot resort to arguments based on coarea formulas in the symmetrized gradient case, which prevents us to adapt the arguments in [15, Remark 1.3.2 and Theorem 3.1.13] to this framework.

The next result collects some basic properties of the quantity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du - v,\Omega )$$\end{document}Vω(Du-v,Ω) given by (5.1).

Theorem 5.6

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn be an open set and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega :\Omega \rightarrow [0,\infty )$$\end{document}ω:Ω→[0,∞) a locally integrable function. Then, the following statements hold: (i)The map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\rightarrow \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du-v,\Omega )$$\end{document}v→Vω(Du-v,Ω) is lower semicontinuous \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym}
