lower-semicontinuous envelope of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω.(iii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}} (\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n) $$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^1(\Omega ; |Du-v|)$$\end{document}ω∈L1(Ω;|Du-v|) is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive, then 5.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathscr {V}_\omega (Du-v,B)=\int _B \omega (x)\;\text {d}|Du-v|(x) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Du-v,B)=∫Bω(x)d|Du-v|(x) for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω.

(i)The map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\rightarrow \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du-v,\Omega )$$\end{document}v→Vω(Du-v,Ω) is lower semicontinuous with respect to the strong convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}} (\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn).

The map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\rightarrow \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du-v,\Omega )$$\end{document}v→Vω(Du-v,Ω) is lower semicontinuous with respect to the strong convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}} (\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn).

(ii)Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du- v,\Omega )=\mathscr {V}_{\omega ^{sc^-}}(Du- v,\Omega )$$\end{document}Vω(Du-v,Ω)=Vωsc-(Du-v,Ω), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega ^{sc^-}$$\end{document}ωsc- denotes the lower-semicontinuous envelope of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω.

Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\text {loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du- v,\Omega )=\mathscr {V}_{\omega ^{sc^-}}(Du- v,\Omega )$$\end{document}Vω(Du-v,Ω)=Vωsc-(Du-v,Ω), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega ^{sc^-}$$\end{document}ωsc- denotes the lower-semicontinuous envelope of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω.

(iii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}} (\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n) $$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^1(\Omega ; |Du-v|)$$\end{document}ω∈L1(Ω;|Du-v|) is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive, then 5.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathscr {V}_\omega (Du-v,B)=\int _B \omega (x)\;\text {d}|Du-v|(x) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Du-v,B)=∫Bω(x)d|Du-v|(x) for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}} (\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n) $$\end{document}v∈Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^1(\Omega ; |Du-v|)$$\end{document}ω∈L1(Ω;|Du-v|) is lower-semicontinuous and strictly positive, then 5.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathscr {V}_\omega (Du-v,B)=\int _B \omega (x)\;\text {d}|Du-v|(x) \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Du-v,B)=∫Bω(x)d|Du-v|(x) for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω.

Proof

To prove (i), let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(v_k)_{k\in \mathbb {N}}\subset L^1_{\omega , \textrm{loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}(vk)k∈N⊂Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn) be a sequence such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_k\rightarrow v$$\end{document}vk→v strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1_{\omega ,\textrm{loc}}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lω,loc1(Ω;Rn). Then, by Definition 5.1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du-v_k,\Omega )\geqslant \int _\Omega \big (u\,\textrm{div}\,\varphi +v_k \cdot \varphi \big ) \;\text {d}{x} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Vω(Du-vk,Ω)⩾∫Ω(udivφ+vk·φ)dxfor every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi \in \textrm{Lip}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}φ∈Lipc(Ω;Rn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\varphi |\leqslant \omega $$\end{document}|φ|⩽ω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω. Moreover, for all such \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi $$\end{document}φ,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \int _\Omega |v_k {-} v||\varphi |\;\text {d}x\leqslant \int _{{{\,\textrm{supp}\,}}\varphi } |v_k - v|\,\omega \;\text {d}x \rightarrow 0 \text { as } k\rightarrow {+}\infty . \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫Ω|vk-v||φ|dx⩽∫suppφ|vk-v|ωdx→0ask→+∞.Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \liminf _{k\rightarrow +\infty }\mathscr {V}_{\omega }(Du-v_k,\Omega )\geqslant \int _\Omega \big (u\,\textrm{div}\,\varphi +v\cdot \varphi \big ) \;\text {d}{x}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim infk→+∞Vω(Du-vk,Ω)⩾∫Ω(udivφ+v·φ)dx,from which the conclusion follows by taking the supremum over all test functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi \in \textrm{Lip}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}φ∈Lipc(Ω;Rn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\varphi |\leqslant \omega $$\end{document}|φ|⩽ω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω.

The proof of (ii) follows by Definition 5.1, observing that every map \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi \in \textrm{Lip}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}φ∈Lipc(Ω;Rn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\varphi |\leqslant \omega $$\end{document}|φ|⩽ω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω also satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\varphi |\leqslant (\omega ^{\textrm{sc}})^-$$\end{document}|φ|⩽(ωsc)- in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω.

As we discuss next, the proof of (iii) is an adaptation of [15, Theorem 2.1.5]. In fact, because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_\omega (\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BVω(Ω) and strictly positive lower-semicontinuous functions are locally bounded away from zero, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_\textrm{loc}(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BVloc(Ω). Then, for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi \in \textrm{Lip}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}φ∈Lipc(Ω;Rn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\varphi |\leqslant \omega $$\end{document}|φ|⩽ω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω, we have that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _\Omega \big (u\,\textrm{div}\,\varphi +v\cdot \varphi \big ) \;\text {d}{x}\leqslant \int _\Omega \omega \;\text {d}|Du-v|; \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫Ω(udivφ+v·φ)dx⩽∫Ωωd|Du-v|;hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {V}_\omega (Du-v,\Omega )\leqslant \int _\Omega \omega \;\text {d}|Du-v|$$\end{document}Vω(Du-v,Ω)⩽∫Ωωd|Du-v|. Conversely, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega \in L^1(\Omega ;|Du-v|)$$\end{document}ω∈L1(Ω;|Du-v|), we infer that5.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\int _{\Omega }\omega \;\text {d}|Du-v|=|\omega (Du-v)|(\Omega )\\&{=}\sup \left\{ \int _\Omega \omega \psi \cdot \;\text {d}(Du-v):\,\psi {\in } \textrm{Lip}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n),\,|\psi |{\leqslant } 1\right\} . \end{aligned}\nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫Ωωd|Du-v|=|ω(Du-v)|(Ω)=sup∫Ωωψ·d(Du-v):ψ∈Lipc(Ω;Rn),|ψ|⩽1.Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega $$\end{document}ω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω as in Example 4.1 (see also [15, Theorem 2.1.2]). Then, for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\psi \in \textrm{Lip}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}ψ∈Lipc(Ω;Rn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\psi |\leqslant 1$$\end{document}|ψ|⩽1 in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _k \, \psi \in \textrm{Lip}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}ωkψ∈Lipc(Ω;Rn) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\omega _k \, \psi |\leqslant \omega _k \leqslant \omega $$\end{document}|ωkψ|⩽ωk⩽ω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω; The proof that this identity holds for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B\subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω can be done exactly as in [15, Theorem 2.1.5]. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 5.7

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_0>0$$\end{document}c0>0 be a positive constant. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \{0,1\},$$\end{document}i∈{0,1}, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i:Q\rightarrow (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}ωi:Q→(0,+∞) be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_0<\inf _Q \omega _i<\sup _Q \omega _i <\frac{1}{c_0}$$\end{document}c0<infQωi<supQωi<1c0, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^1_{\omega _0,\textrm{loc}}(Q)$$\end{document}u∈Lω0,loc1(Q). Then, for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega _1}(Q;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}v∈L11(Q;Rn), we have5.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert v\Vert _{L^1_{\omega _1}(Q;\mathbb {R}^n)}\leqslant \frac{1}{c_0^2} \big (\mathscr {V}_{\omega _0}(Du-v,Q)+TV_{\omega _0}(u,Q)\big ).\nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖v‖L11(Q;Rn)⩽1c02(Vω0(Du-v,Q)+TVω0(u,Q)).

Proof

Fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v\in L^1_{\omega _1}(Q;\mathbb {R}^2)$$\end{document}v∈L11(Q;R2). Note that the uniform bounds on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _1$$\end{document}ω1 yield5.12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} c_0\int _Q |v(x)| \;\text {d}x&\leqslant \int _Q \omega _1(x) |v(x)| \;\text {d}x = \Vert v\Vert _{L^1_{\omega _1}(Q;\mathbb {R}^n)} \\&\leqslant \frac{1}{c_0} \int _Q |v(x)| \;\text {d}x. \end{aligned}\nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}c0∫Q|v(x)|dx⩽∫Qω1(x)|v(x)|dx=‖v‖L11(Q;Rn)⩽1c0∫Q|v(x)|dx.In particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v \in L^1(Q;\mathbb {R}^2)$$\end{document}v∈L1(Q;R2); thus,5.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&c_0\int _Q |v(x)| \;\text {d}x \\&= c_0\sup \bigg \{ \int _Q \psi (x) \cdot v(x)\;\text {d}x: \psi \in \textrm{Lip}_{\textrm{c}}(Q;\mathbb {R}^2), \\ &\Vert \psi \Vert _{L^\infty (Q;\mathbb {R}^2)} \leqslant 1 \bigg \} \\&= \sup \bigg \{ \int _Q \tilde{\psi }(x) \cdot v(x)\;\text {d}x: \tilde{\psi }\in \textrm{Lip}_{\textrm{c}}(Q;\mathbb {R}^2), \\ &\Vert \tilde{\psi }\Vert _{L^\infty (Q;\mathbb {R}^2)} \leqslant c_0 \bigg \} \\&\leqslant \sup \bigg \{\int _Q \varphi (x) \cdot v(x)\;\text {d}x: \\ &\varphi \in \textrm{Lip}_{\textrm{c}}(Q;\mathbb {R}^2),\,\, |\varphi |\leqslant \omega _0\bigg \}\\&\leqslant \mathscr {V}_{\omega _0}(Du-v,Q)+TV_{\omega _0}(u,Q), \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}c0∫Q|v(x)|dx=c0sup{∫Qψ(x)·v(x)dx:ψ∈Lipc(Q;R2),‖ψ‖L∞(Q;R2)⩽1}=sup{∫Qψ~(x)·v(x)dx:ψ~∈Lipc(Q;R2),‖ψ~‖L∞(Q;R2)⩽c0}⩽sup{∫Qφ(x)·v(x)dx:φ∈Lipc(Q;R2),|φ|⩽ω0}⩽Vω0(Du-v,Q)+TVω0(u,Q),where we used Definition 5.1 together with the subadditivity of the supremum in the last estimate, and the bound \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_0 \leqslant \inf _Q \omega _0 $$\end{document}c0⩽infQω0 in the preceding one. We then obtain (5.11) by combining (5.12) and (5.13). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Under the same assumptions of Lemma 5.7, the infimum problem in (1.24) is actually a minimum.

Lemma 5.8

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_0>0$$\end{document}c0>0 be a positive constant. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i\in \{0,1\},$$\end{document}i∈{0,1}, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega _i:Q\rightarrow (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}ωi:Q→(0,+∞) be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_0<\inf _Q \omega _i<\sup _Q \omega _i <\frac{1}{c_0}$$\end{document}c0<infQωi<supQωi<1c0, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^1(Q)$$\end{document}u∈L1(Q). Then, there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*\in BD_{\omega _1}(Q)$$\end{document}u∗∈BDω1(Q) such that5.14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} TGV_{\omega _0,\omega _1}(u,Q)=\mathscr {V}_{\omega _0}(Du-u^*, Q)+\mathscr {V}_{\omega _1}(\mathcal {E}u^*, Q). \end{aligned}\nonumber \\ \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGVω0,ω1(u,Q)=Vω0(Du-u∗,Q)+Vω1(Eu∗,Q).

Proof

We claim that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{\omega _0,\omega _1}(u,Q)$$\end{document}TGVω0,ω1(u,Q) is finite if and only if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV_{\omega _0}(Q)$$\end{document}u∈BVω0(Q). In fact, choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v=0$$\end{document}v=0 as a competitor in (5.10), we infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{\omega _0,\omega _1}(u,Q)\leqslant TV_{\omega _0}(u,Q)$$\end{document}TGVω0,ω1(u,Q)⩽TVω0(u,Q). On the other hand, recalling (3.3), we have for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ v\in BD_{\omega _1}(Q) $$\end{document}v∈BDω1(Q) that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TV_{\omega _0}(u,Q)&=\sup \Bigg \{\int _Q (u \, {\text {div}}\,\varphi +v \cdot \varphi -v\cdot \varphi ) \;\text {d}x \\ &:\,\varphi \in {\text {Lip}}_c(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^2),\,|\varphi |\leqslant \omega _0\Bigg \}\\&\leqslant \mathscr {V}_{\omega _0}(Du-v, Q)+\Vert v\Vert _{L^1_{\omega _0}(Q;\mathbb {R}^2)}\\&\leqslant \mathscr {V}_{\omega _0}(Du-v, Q)+\frac{1}{c_0^2}\Vert v\Vert _{ L^1_{\omega _1}(Q;\mathbb {R}^2)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TVω0(u,Q)=sup{∫Q(udivφ+v·φ-v·φ)dx:φ∈Lipc(Ω;R2),|φ|⩽ω0}⩽Vω0(Du-v,Q)+‖v‖L01(Q;R2)⩽Vω0(Du-v,Q)+1c02‖v‖L11(Q;R2),where we used the subadditivity of the supremum combined with Definition 5.1 in the first inequality, and the bounds on the two weights in the second inequality. Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TV_{\omega _0}(u,Q) \leqslant \max \{1, c_0^{-2}\} \, TGV_{\omega _0,\omega _1}(u,Q)$$\end{document}TVω0(u,Q)⩽max{1,c0-2}TGVω0,ω1(u,Q), which concludes the proof of the claim.

On Level \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1$$\end{document}1

As we address next, and similarly to the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TV\!}_{\omega }}$$\end{document}(LS)TVω case, the box constraint provides a stopping criterion for the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV$$\end{document}TGV-learning scheme.

To proceed as in Theorem 3.6, we need an analog to Proposition 3.5, which we now prove. Lemma 5.10

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\subset Q$$\end{document}L⊂Q be a dyadic square. Then, there is a constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_Q^{rot} > 0$$\end{document}CQrot>0 such that for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in BV(L)$$\end{document}u∈BV(L) and for every skew-symmetric matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M\in \mathbb {R}^{2\times 2}_{\text {skew}}$$\end{document}M∈Rskew2×2, we have5.20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C_Q^{rot} |Du|(L) \leqslant |Du - R_M|(L). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}CQrot|Du|(L)⩽|Du-RM|(L).

Proof

Suppose that (5.20) does not hold; then, we may find functions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_n \in BV(L)$$\end{document}un∈BV(L) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|Du_n|(L) = 1$$\end{document}|Dun|(L)=1 and skew-symmetric matrices \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_n \in \mathbb {R}^{2\times 2}_{\text {skew}}$$\end{document}Mn∈Rskew2×2 for which5.21\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{n}=\frac{1}{n}|Du_n|(L) > |Du_n - R_{M_n}|(L). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1n=1n|Dun|(L)>|Dun-RMn|(L).Then, in particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert R_{M_n}\Vert _{L^1(L)}\leqslant 2$$\end{document}‖RMn‖L1(L)⩽2; consequently, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^{2 \times 2}_{\text {skew}}$$\end{document}Rskew2×2 is a finite-dimensional set, we can assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R_{M_n} \rightarrow R_{M_\infty }$$\end{document}RMn→RM∞ for some skew-symmetric matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_\infty $$\end{document}M∞, up to taking a not relabelled subsequence.

On the other hand, recalling (5.18), there are constants \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_n \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}cn∈R satisfying\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u_n - c_n\Vert _{L^2(L)} \leqslant C^{BV}_Q |Du_n|(L); \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖un-cn‖L2(L)⩽CQBV|Dun|(L);thus, up to taking a not relabelled further subsequence, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_n - c_n {\mathop {\rightharpoonup }\limits ^{*}} u_\infty \in BV(L)$$\end{document}un-cn⇀∗u∞∈BV(L) for some \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_\infty \in BV(L)$$\end{document}u∞∈BV(L). Using (5.21) once more, we must have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du_\infty = R_{M_\infty }$$\end{document}Du∞=RM∞. At this point, we can distinguish two cases, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_\infty = 0$$\end{document}M∞=0 or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_\infty \not =0$$\end{document}M∞≠0.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_\infty = 0$$\end{document}M∞=0, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{n}=\frac{1}{n}|Du_n|(L) > |Du_n - R_{M_n}|(L) \rightarrow 1, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1n=1n|Dun|(L)>|Dun-RMn|(L)→1,which cannot be.

\setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$M_\infty \ne 0$$\end{document}M∞≠0, then, using the antisymmetry of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} }$$\end{document}DRM∞=M∞, we again arrive at a contradiction, since\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C_L |Du|(L) \leqslant |Du - R_M|(L) \quad \text { for all }M \in \mathbb {R}^{2 \times 2}_{\text {skew}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}CL|Du|(L)⩽|Du-RM|(L)for allM∈Rskew2×2.To see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

The next proposition guarantees that if a dyadic square \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\subset Q$$\end{document}L⊂Q is small enough, then a solution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _0,\alpha _1}$$\end{document}uα0,α1 of Level 3 of our \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV$$\end{document}TGV learning scheme in (1.20) is affine for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _0,\alpha _1) \in \big [c_0,\frac{1}{c_0}\big ]\times \big [c_1,\frac{1}{c_1}\big ]$$\end{document}(α0,α1)∈[c0,1c0]×[c1,1c1]. Let us remark that a related result is contained in [57, Proposition 6], which we make quantitative and with a scaling that enables us to draw conclusions on the cell size.

Proposition 5.11

Fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_0$$\end{document}c0, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_1>0$$\end{document}c1>0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\subset Q$$\end{document}L⊂Q a dyadic square. If5.22\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert u_\eta \Vert _{L^2(L)} < \min \left( c_0, \frac{c_1}{C_Q^{BD}|L|^{1/2}}\right) \frac{C_Q^{rot}}{C_Q^{BV}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖uη‖L2(L)<minc0,c1CQBD|L|1/2CQrotCQBV,then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_L:=(\overline{\alpha }_0, \overline{\alpha }_1 ) = (c_0, c_1)$$\end{document}α¯L:=(α¯0,α¯1)=(c0,c1) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }_L }:= u_{(\overline{\alpha }_0, \overline{\alpha }_1),L}$$\end{document}uα¯L:=u(α¯0,α¯1),L is affine on L, with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }_L } = \langle u_\eta \rangle _L$$\end{document}uα¯L=⟨uη⟩L.

Proof

To simplify the notation in the proof, we omit the dependence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{\alpha _0,\alpha _1}$$\end{document}TGVα0,α1 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u_{{\alpha }_0, {\alpha }_1}$$\end{document}uα0,α1 on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L$$\end{document}L by writing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{\alpha _0,\alpha _1}(\cdot )$$\end{document}TGVα0,α1(·) in place of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{\alpha _0,\alpha _1}(\cdot ,L)$$\end{document}TGVα0,α1(·,L) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u_{({\alpha }_0, {\alpha }_1),L}$$\end{document}u(α0,α1),L, respectively.

Fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _0,\alpha _1) \in \big [c_0,\frac{1}{c_0}\big ]\times \big [c_1,\frac{1}{c_1}\big ]$$\end{document}(α0,α1)∈[c0,1c0]×[c1,1c1]. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_L = (c_0,c_1)$$\end{document}α¯L=(c0,c1), and this concludes the proof. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Owing to Proposition 5.11, we are now in a position to reduce the minimum problem in Level 1 of our training scheme to a minimization over a finite set of admissible partitions.

Theorem 5.12

Consider the learning scheme \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TGV\!}_{\omega }}$$\end{document}(LS)TGVω in (1.20) with (1.21) restricted by (1.29) (see (5.3)). Then, there exist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\kappa \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}κ∈N and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {L}_1,..., \mathscr {L}_\kappa \in \mathscr {P}$$\end{document}L1,...,Lκ∈P such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&{\text {argmin}}\left\{ \int _Q|u_c-u_{\mathscr {L}}|^2\;\text {d}x:\,\mathscr {L}\in \mathscr {P}\right\} \\&\quad = {\text {argmin}}\left\{ \int _Q|u_c-u_{\mathscr {L}_i}|^2\;\text {d}x:\,i\in \{1,...,\kappa \}\right\} . \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}argmin∫Q|uc-uL|2dx:L∈P=argmin∫Q|uc-uLi|2dx:i∈{1,...,κ}.

Proof

The proof is analogous to that of Theorem 3.6, so we only provide a sketch of the argument. The only difference here is that instead of being a constant, the solution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ u_{\alpha ,L}$$\end{document}uα,L of Level 1 is affine for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha :=(\alpha _0,\alpha _1)\in \big [c_0,\frac{1}{c_0}\big ]\times \big [c_1,\frac{1}{c_1}\big ]$$\end{document}α:=(α0,α1)∈[c0,1c0]×[c1,1c1] on squares L on which (5.22) holds, due to Proposition 5.11. Moreover, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{\alpha _0,\alpha _1}( u_{\alpha ,L},L)=0$$\end{document}TGVα0,α1(uα,L,L)=0 and, recalling (5.3), the optimal parameter given by (5.3) is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_L=(c_0,c_1)$$\end{document}α¯L=(c0,c1). As in the proof of Theorem 3.6, this observation allows us to replace any partition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {L}^*$$\end{document}L∗ containing such small dyadic squares with another partition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\overline{\mathscr {L}}^*$$\end{document}L¯∗ whose dyadic squares have all side length above the threshold provided by (5.22) without affecting the minimizer of Level 2. We refer to Fig. 1 for a graphical idea of the argument and to Theorem 3.6 for the details of the proof.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We conclude this section by proving existence of an optimal solution to the learning scheme \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$({\mathscr {L}}\!{\mathscr {S}})_{{TGV\!}_\omega }$$\end{document}(LS)TGVω.

Proof of Theorem 1.7

The result follows directly by combining the analysis in Subsect. 5.1, Proposition 5.9, and Theorem 5.12.\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Stopping Criteria and Box Constraint for TGV

In this subsection, we prove a TGV-counterpart to Theorem 3.8. Our result reads as follows.

\usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,+\infty \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ c_\Omega \leqslant \min \{(\alpha ^*_\Omega )_0,(\alpha ^*_\Omega )_1\} < C_\Omega \Vert u_\eta \Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}$$\end{document}cΩ⩽min{(αΩ∗)0,(αΩ∗)1}<CΩ‖uη‖L2(Ω).

There exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\hat{\alpha }\in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α^∈(0,+∞)2 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{\hat{\alpha }_0,\hat{\alpha }_1}(u_c,\Omega ) < TGV_{\hat{\alpha }_0,\hat{\alpha }_1}(u_\eta ,\Omega )$$\end{document}TGVα^0,α^1(uc,Ω)<TGVα^0,α^1(uη,Ω);

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\displaystyle \Vert u_\eta - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} <\Vert \langle u_\eta \rangle - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} $$\end{document}‖uη-uc‖L2(Ω)2<‖⟨uη⟩-uc‖L2(Ω)2.

In particular, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega =L$$\end{document}Ω=L with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L\subset Q$$\end{document}L⊂Q is a dyadic square, then there exists a positive constant, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_L$$\end{document}cL, such that any minimizer, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha ^*_L$$\end{document}αL∗, of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{J} $$\end{document}J^ over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,+\infty ]^2$$\end{document}[0,+∞]2 satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ c_L\leqslant \min \{(\alpha ^*_L)_0,(\alpha ^*_L)_1\} < C_Q\Vert u_\eta \Vert _{L^2(L)}$$\end{document}cL⩽min{(αL∗)0,(αL∗)1}<CQ‖uη‖L2(L), where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_Q$$\end{document}CQ is a constant given by Proposition 5.11.

Owing to the orthogonality property (5.5), condition ii) in the statement of the theorem is equivalent to requiring that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert u_c-\langle u_c \rangle -u_\eta +\langle u_\eta \rangle \Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}^2\leqslant \Vert u_c-\langle u_c \rangle \Vert _{L^2(\Omega )}^2.$$\end{document}‖uc-⟨uc⟩-uη+⟨uη⟩‖L2(Ω)2⩽‖uc-⟨uc⟩‖L2(Ω)2. In other words, ii) is satisfied provided that the perturbation which the noise causes on the non-affine portion of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_c$$\end{document}uc is small in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-sense compared to the original non-affine component of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_c$$\end{document}uc. This is the case, for example, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta =u_\eta -u_c$$\end{document}η=uη-uc and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta -\langle \eta \rangle $$\end{document}η-⟨η⟩ has a small \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-norm, regardless of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-norm of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle \eta \rangle $$\end{document}⟨η⟩.

We remark that the conclusion of the theorem in the general case is slightly weaker than the corresponding result for the TV-setting. Indeed, while we can show that both entries of optimal parameters must be uniformly bounded away from zero, we can only prove that their minimum is uniformly bounded from above but cannot prevent that just one of the entries blows up to infinity. This is due to the fact that, without additional conditions, the maps \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_\alpha $$\end{document}uα are not necessarily affine if just one of the entries of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha $$\end{document}α becomes infinity, cf. also [57, Proposition 6] for comparison.

However, as a direct consequence of our result, we find a complete characterization for the case in which the analysis of TGV reduces to a one-dimensional problem.

Corollary 5.14

As in the case of the total variation, we proceed by first studying the limiting behavior of the sum of fidelity and TGV-seminorm in the sense of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence. Lemma 5.15

Proof

The thesis follows then by a classical diagonalization argument.(iii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=+\infty \text { and }\bar{\alpha }_1=\alpha _1\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0=+∞andα¯1=α1∈(0,+∞), then we can assume, without loss of generality, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BH(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BH(Ω). In particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla u \in BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}∇u∈BD(Ω) which we can then use as a competitor in the definition of TGV to infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_1 |D^2u|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)1|D2u|(Ω). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u] & \leqslant \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }\bigg (\int _\Omega |u_\eta -u|^2\;\text {d}x\\ & \quad +(\alpha _j)_1|D^2 u|(\Omega )\bigg )=G_{\infty ,\alpha _1} [u]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[u]⩽lim supj→+∞(∫Ω|uη-u|2dx+(αj)1|D2u|(Ω))=G∞,α1[u].(iv)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0\in (0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}α¯0∈(0,+∞] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=0$$\end{document}α¯1=0, arguing by approximation as in case (ii), we can assume without loss of generality that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in C^\infty _c(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈Cc∞(Ω). Then, choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla u$$\end{document}∇u as a competitor in the definition of TGV, we find that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_1 |\nabla ^2 u|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)1|∇2u|(Ω). Hence, arguing as in case (ii) once more, yields (5.32).(v)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\alpha _0\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0=α0∈(0,+∞) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯1=+∞, then we can assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BV(Ω). Choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m_\mathcal {E}(Du)$$\end{document}mE(Du) in the definition of TGV, yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_0 |D u-m_\mathcal E(Du)|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)0|Du-mE(Du)|(Ω). Hence, arguing as in case (ii), we infer (5.32).(vi)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then, without loss of generality, we can assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du\in {\text {Ker}}\,\mathcal {E}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^d)$$\end{document}Du∈KerE(Ω;Rd). Choosing Du as a competitor in the definition of TGV shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)=0$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)=0 for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}j∈N, from which (5.32) follows.The \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(G_{\alpha _j})_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Gαj)j∈N to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}Gα¯ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}L1(Ω) is then a direct consequence of (5.30) and (5.32). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

(i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, then an argument by contradiction as the classical TGV case and variants thereof (see, e.g., [29, Proposition 5.3]) yields uniform bounds for sequences \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uj)j∈N and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_j^*)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uj∗)j∈N in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}BD(Ω), respectively. Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BV(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_j\rightharpoonup u$$\end{document}uj⇀u weakly-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω). (ii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=0$$\end{document}α¯0=0, then (5.30) holds by the lower-semicontinuity of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-norm with respect to the weak convergence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω).

(iv)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0\in (0,\infty ]$$\end{document}α¯0∈(0,∞] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=0$$\end{document}α¯1=0, then the situation is analogous to (ii).
