\usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uj)j∈N is uniformly bounded in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω), so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_j\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup }u$$\end{document}uj⇀∗u weakly-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω). The statement follows from the lower semicontinuity of the total variation with respect to the weak-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ convergence of measures, as well as from (5.29).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯1=+∞ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0∈(0,+∞), then there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*$$\end{document}u∗ affine and such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*_j\rightarrow u^*$$\end{document}uj∗→u∗ strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}BD(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(uj)j∈N is uniformly bounded in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω), so that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_j\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup }u$$\end{document}uj⇀∗u weakly-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω). The statement follows from the lower semicontinuity of the total variation with respect to the weak-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ convergence of measures, as well as from (5.29).

(vi)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*\in {\text {Ker}}\,\mathcal {E}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^d)$$\end{document}u∗∈KerE(Ω;Rd) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*_j\rightarrow u^*$$\end{document}uj∗→u∗ strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}BD(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du_j\rightarrow u^*$$\end{document}Duj→u∗ strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_b(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^d)$$\end{document}Mb(Ω;Rd). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du=u^*$$\end{document}Du=u∗ and the statement follows.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*\in {\text {Ker}}\,\mathcal {E}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^d)$$\end{document}u∗∈KerE(Ω;Rd) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*_j\rightarrow u^*$$\end{document}uj∗→u∗ strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}BD(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du_j\rightarrow u^*$$\end{document}Duj→u∗ strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {M}_b(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^d)$$\end{document}Mb(Ω;Rd). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du=u^*$$\end{document}Du=u∗ and the statement follows.

(i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, then we can assume, without loss of generality, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BV(Ω). The conclusion follows then by a classical argument relying on the continuity of TGV with respect to its tuning parameters (see, e.g., [29, Theorem 4.2]).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, then we can assume, without loss of generality, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BV(Ω). The conclusion follows then by a classical argument relying on the continuity of TGV with respect to its tuning parameters (see, e.g., [29, Theorem 4.2]).

(ii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=0$$\end{document}α¯0=0, then we consider for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈L2(Ω) an approximating sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_k)_{k\in \mathbb {N}}\subset C^\infty _c(\Omega )$$\end{document}(uk)k∈N⊂Cc∞(Ω) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_k\rightarrow u$$\end{document}uk→u strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω). Choosing the null function as a competitor in the definition of TGV, we find that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u_k)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_0 |D u_k|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(uk)⩽(αj)0|Duk|(Ω). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u_k] = G_{0,\bar{\alpha }_1}[u_k] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limj→+∞Gαj[uk]=G0,α¯1[uk] for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1\in [0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}α¯1∈[0,+∞] and every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}k∈N. The thesis follows then by a classical diagonalization argument.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=0$$\end{document}α¯0=0, then we consider for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈L2(Ω) an approximating sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_k)_{k\in \mathbb {N}}\subset C^\infty _c(\Omega )$$\end{document}(uk)k∈N⊂Cc∞(Ω) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_k\rightarrow u$$\end{document}uk→u strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω). Choosing the null function as a competitor in the definition of TGV, we find that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u_k)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_0 |D u_k|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(uk)⩽(αj)0|Duk|(Ω). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u_k] = G_{0,\bar{\alpha }_1}[u_k] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limj→+∞Gαj[uk]=G0,α¯1[uk] for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1\in [0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}α¯1∈[0,+∞] and every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}k∈N. The thesis follows then by a classical diagonalization argument.

(iii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=+\infty \text { and }\bar{\alpha }_1=\alpha _1\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0=+∞andα¯1=α1∈(0,+∞), then we can assume, without loss of generality, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BH(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BH(Ω). In particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla u \in BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}∇u∈BD(Ω) which we can then use as a competitor in the definition of TGV to infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_1 |D^2u|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)1|D2u|(Ω). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u] & \leqslant \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }\bigg (\int _\Omega |u_\eta -u|^2\;\text {d}x\\ & \quad +(\alpha _j)_1|D^2 u|(\Omega )\bigg )=G_{\infty ,\alpha _1} [u]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[u]⩽lim supj→+∞(∫Ω|uη-u|2dx+(αj)1|D2u|(Ω))=G∞,α1[u].

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=+\infty \text { and }\bar{\alpha }_1=\alpha _1\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0=+∞andα¯1=α1∈(0,+∞), then we can assume, without loss of generality, that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BH(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BH(Ω). In particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla u \in BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}∇u∈BD(Ω) which we can then use as a competitor in the definition of TGV to infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_1 |D^2u|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)1|D2u|(Ω). Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u] & \leqslant \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }\bigg (\int _\Omega |u_\eta -u|^2\;\text {d}x\\ & \quad +(\alpha _j)_1|D^2 u|(\Omega )\bigg )=G_{\infty ,\alpha _1} [u]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[u]⩽lim supj→+∞(∫Ω|uη-u|2dx+(αj)1|D2u|(Ω))=G∞,α1[u].

(iv)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0\in (0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}α¯0∈(0,+∞] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=0$$\end{document}α¯1=0, arguing by approximation as in case (ii), we can assume without loss of generality that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in C^\infty _c(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈Cc∞(Ω). Then, choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla u$$\end{document}∇u as a competitor in the definition of TGV, we find that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_1 |\nabla ^2 u|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)1|∇2u|(Ω). Hence, arguing as in case (ii) once more, yields (5.32).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0\in (0,+\infty ]$$\end{document}α¯0∈(0,+∞] and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=0$$\end{document}α¯1=0, arguing by approximation as in case (ii), we can assume without loss of generality that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in C^\infty _c(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈Cc∞(Ω). Then, choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nabla u$$\end{document}∇u as a competitor in the definition of TGV, we find that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_1 |\nabla ^2 u|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)1|∇2u|(Ω). Hence, arguing as in case (ii) once more, yields (5.32).

(v)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\alpha _0\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0=α0∈(0,+∞) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯1=+∞, then we can assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BV(Ω). Choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m_\mathcal {E}(Du)$$\end{document}mE(Du) in the definition of TGV, yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_0 |D u-m_\mathcal E(Du)|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)0|Du-mE(Du)|(Ω). Hence, arguing as in case (ii), we infer (5.32).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\alpha _0\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0=α0∈(0,+∞) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯1=+∞, then we can assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BV(Ω). Choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m_\mathcal {E}(Du)$$\end{document}mE(Du) in the definition of TGV, yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)\leqslant (\alpha _j)_0 |D u-m_\mathcal E(Du)|(\Omega ). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)⩽(αj)0|Du-mE(Du)|(Ω). Hence, arguing as in case (ii), we infer (5.32).

(vi)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then, without loss of generality, we can assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du\in {\text {Ker}}\,\mathcal {E}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^d)$$\end{document}Du∈KerE(Ω;Rd). Choosing Du as a competitor in the definition of TGV shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)=0$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)=0 for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}j∈N, from which (5.32) follows.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then, without loss of generality, we can assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Du\in {\text {Ker}}\,\mathcal {E}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^d)$$\end{document}Du∈KerE(Ω;Rd). Choosing Du as a competitor in the definition of TGV shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u)=0$$\end{document}TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(u)=0 for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}j∈N, from which (5.32) follows.

As a consequence of the previous result, we provide a characterization of the unique minimizer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}uα¯ of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}Gα¯.

Corollary 5.16

Under the same assumptions of Lemma 5.15, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}:={\text {argmin}}_{u\in L^1(\Omega )} G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u]$$\end{document}uα¯:=argminu∈L1(Ω)Gα¯[u] for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }\in [0,+\infty ]^2$$\end{document}α¯∈[0,+∞]2. Then,5.33\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} u_{\bar{\alpha }}={\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} u_{\alpha }& \text {if }\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2,\\ u_\eta & \text {if }\bar{\alpha }_0=0\text { or }\bar{\alpha }_1=0,\\ \langle u_\eta \rangle _\Omega & \text {if }\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty . \end{array}\right. } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}uα¯=uαifα¯=α∈(0,+∞)2,uηifα¯0=0orα¯1=0,⟨uη⟩Ωifα¯0=α¯1=+∞.Additionally, when just one among \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0$$\end{document}α¯0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1$$\end{document}α¯1 is infinite, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\langle u_{\bar{\alpha }}\rangle _\Omega =\langle u_{\eta }\rangle _\Omega $$\end{document}⟨uα¯⟩Ω=⟨uη⟩Ω. In these regimes, if additionally \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_\eta =\langle u_{\eta }\rangle _\Omega $$\end{document}uη=⟨uη⟩Ω, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}=\langle u_{\bar{\alpha }}\rangle _\Omega $$\end{document}uα¯=⟨uα¯⟩Ω.

Proof

The first claim follows directly from Lemma 5.15. We show the second statement only in the case in which \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=∞ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1$$\end{document}α¯1 is finite, being the case in which \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=\infty $$\end{document}α¯1=∞ analogous. The characterization of minimizers is then a consequence of the orthogonality property in (5.5) which, in turn, yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\infty ,\bar{\alpha }_1}(u) & =\int _\Omega |\langle u-u_\eta \rangle _\Omega |^2 \;\text {d}x \\ & \quad +\int _\Omega \left[ \left( u-\langle u\rangle _\Omega \right) -\left( u_\eta -\langle u_\eta \rangle _\Omega \right) \right] ^2 \;\text {d}x\\ & \quad \, +\, \bar{\alpha }_1| D^2 \left( u-\langle u\rangle _\Omega \right) | \end{aligned}$$\end{document}G∞,α¯1(u)=∫Ω|⟨u-uη⟩Ω|2dx+∫Ωu-⟨u⟩Ω-uη-⟨uη⟩Ω2dx+α¯1|D2u-⟨u⟩Ω|for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u\in BH(\Omega )$$\end{document}u∈BH(Ω). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Lemma 5.17

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}Ω⊂R2 be a bounded, Lipschitz domain and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(G_{\bar{\alpha }})_{\bar{\alpha }\in [0,+\infty ]}$$\end{document}(Gα¯)α¯∈[0,+∞] be the family of functionals introduced in Lemma 5.15. Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }\in [0,\infty ]^2$$\end{document}α¯∈[0,∞]2, set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}:= {\text {argmin}}_{u\in L^1(\Omega )} G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u]$$\end{document}uα¯:=argminu∈L1(Ω)Gα¯[u]. Proof

With the same notation as in the proof of Lemma 5.15, we detail the argument for each case separately. (i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, then the statement follows directly by choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j=\alpha $$\end{document}αj=α for every j.(ii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=0$$\end{document}α¯0=0, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}=u_\eta $$\end{document}uα¯=uη and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\eta }]=0$$\end{document}Gα¯[uα¯]=Gα¯[uη]=0. In view of Lemma 5.15, there exists a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_\eta ^j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset L^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}(uηj)j∈N⊂L1(Ω) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u^j_\eta ]\leqslant G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_\eta ]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[uηj]⩽Gα¯[uη]. Hence, for any sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞)2 satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j\rightarrow \bar{\alpha }$$\end{document}αj→α¯, the minimality of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}uαj yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned}&\limsup _{j\rightarrow \infty } \bigg ( \int _\Omega |u_{\alpha _j} - u_{\eta }|^2\;\text {d}x+ TGV_{(\alpha _j)_0,(\alpha _j)_1}(u_{\alpha _j},\Omega )\bigg )\\&=\limsup _{j\rightarrow \infty } G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]\\ &\leqslant \limsup _{j\rightarrow \infty } G_{\alpha _j}[u^j_\eta ]\leqslant G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_\eta ]=0. \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→∞(∫Ω|uαj-uη|2dx+TGV(αj)0,(αj)1(uαj,Ω))=lim supj→∞Gαj[uαj]⩽lim supj→∞Gαj[uηj]⩽Gα¯[uη]=0. _1)$$\end{document}αj:=(α0j,α1), from the minimality of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} }}$$\end{document}∇uα¯ as a competitor in the definition of TGV, we find \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]\leqslant \int _{\Omega }|u_\eta -u_\eta ^k|^2\,\;\text {d}x \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[uαj]⩽∫Ω|uη-uηk|2dx for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}k∈N. Passing to the limit as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\rightarrow +\infty $$\end{document}k→+∞, we infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]=0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[uαj]=0. In turn, this implies (5.34).(v)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\alpha _0\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯0=α0∈(0,+∞) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯1=+∞, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}\in BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}uα¯∈BV(Ω). For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _1^j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}(α1j)j∈N⊂(0,+∞) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _1^j\rightarrow +\infty $$\end{document}α1j→+∞, and setting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j:=(\alpha _0,\alpha _1^j)$$\end{document}αj:=(α0,α1j), we deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]&\leqslant G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]\leqslant \int _{\Omega }|u_{\bar{\alpha }}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x\\ &\quad +\alpha _0|D u_{\bar{\alpha }}-m_{\mathcal {E}}(Du_{\bar{\alpha }})|(\Omega )=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Gαj[uαj]⩽Gαj[uα¯]⩽∫Ω|uα¯-uη|2dx+α0|Duα¯-mE(Duα¯)|(Ω)=Gα¯[uα¯]. By the fundamental theorem of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence, we infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j}\rightarrow u_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}uαj→uα¯ strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}L1(Ω) and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]\rightarrow G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}].$$\end{document}Gαj[uαj]→Gα¯[uα¯]. On the other hand, letting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}uj∗ be defined as in (5.31) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_j$$\end{document}uj replaced by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}uαj, the same argument as in item (v) in the first part of the proof of Lemma 5.15 yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j}\overset{*}{\rightharpoonup }u$$\end{document}uαj⇀∗u weakly-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*_{\alpha _j}\rightarrow u^*$$\end{document}uj∗→u∗ strongly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}BD(Ω) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} By combining the above convergences, we deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]&\leqslant \int _\Omega |u_{\bar{\alpha }}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x\\&+\alpha _0|Du_{\bar{\alpha }}-u^*|(\Omega ) \leqslant \liminf _{j\rightarrow +\infty }\int _\Omega |u_{\alpha _j}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x \\ &\quad +\alpha _0| Du_{\alpha _j}-u^*_{\alpha _j}|(\Omega )\leqslant \lim _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u_{ \alpha _j }]\\&=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}], \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Gα¯[uα¯]⩽∫Ω|uα¯-uη|2dx+α0|Duα¯-u∗|(Ω)⩽lim infj→+∞∫Ω|uαj-uη|2dx+α0|Duαj-uj∗|(Ω)⩽limj→+∞Gαj[uαj]=Gα¯[uα¯], where the first inequality follows by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m_{\mathcal E}$$\end{document}mE, cf. (5.29), whereas the second one is a consequence of the lower semicontinuity of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-norm with respect to the weak \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-convergence, as well as of the lower semicontinuity of the total variation with respect to the weak-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ convergence of measures.(vi)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}uα¯ is affine. Thus, for every sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}} \subset (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞)2 satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j\rightarrow \bar{\alpha }$$\end{document}αj→α¯, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]\leqslant G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]=\int _\Omega |u_{\bar{\alpha }}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Gαj[uαj]⩽Gαj[uα¯]=∫Ω|uα¯-uη|2dx=Gα¯[uα¯]. Property (5.34) is once again obtained arguing by the fundamental theorem of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence, as in (iii).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

(i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, then the statement follows directly by choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j=\alpha $$\end{document}αj=α for every j.

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, then the statement follows directly by choosing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j=\alpha $$\end{document}αj=α for every j.

(ii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=0$$\end{document}α¯0=0, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}=u_\eta $$\end{document}uα¯=uη and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\eta }]=0$$\end{document}Gα¯[uα¯]=Gα¯[uη]=0. In view of Lemma 5.15, there exists a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_\eta ^j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset L^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}(uηj)j∈N⊂L1(Ω) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u^j_\eta ]\leqslant G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_\eta ]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[uηj]⩽Gα¯[uη]. Thus, we infer (5.34).

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=0$$\end{document}α¯0=0, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}=u_\eta $$\end{document}uα¯=uη and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\eta }]=0$$\end{document}Gα¯[uα¯]=Gα¯[uη]=0. In view of Lemma 5.15, there exists a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(u_\eta ^j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset L^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}(uηj)j∈N⊂L1(Ω) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u^j_\eta ]\leqslant G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_\eta ]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supj→+∞Gαj[uηj]⩽Gα¯[uη]. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=+∞ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_1=\alpha _1\in (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}α¯1=α1∈(0,+∞), then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}\in BH(\Omega )$$\end{document}uα¯∈BH(Ω). \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\rightarrow +\infty $$\end{document}j→+∞, setting \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BD(\Omega )$$\end{document}BD(Ω) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^*$$\end{document}u∗ affine. By combining the above convergences, we deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]&\leqslant \int _\Omega |u_{\bar{\alpha }}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x\\&+\alpha _0|Du_{\bar{\alpha }}-u^*|(\Omega ) \leqslant \liminf _{j\rightarrow +\infty }\int _\Omega |u_{\alpha _j}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x \\ &\quad +\alpha _0| Du_{\alpha _j}-u^*_{\alpha _j}|(\Omega )\leqslant \lim _{j\rightarrow +\infty }G_{\alpha _j}[u_{ \alpha _j }]\\&=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}], \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Gα¯[uα¯]⩽∫Ω|uα¯-uη|2dx+α0|Duα¯-u∗|(Ω)⩽lim infj→+∞∫Ω|uαj-uη|2dx+α0|Duαj-uj∗|(Ω)⩽limj→+∞Gαj[uαj]=Gα¯[uα¯], where the first inequality follows by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m_{\mathcal E}$$\end{document}mE, cf. (5.29), whereas the second one is a consequence of the lower semicontinuity of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-norm with respect to the weak \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2-convergence, as well as of the lower semicontinuity of the total variation with respect to the weak-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\star $$\end{document}⋆ convergence of measures.

(vi)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}uα¯ is affine. Thus, for every sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}} \subset (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞)2 satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j\rightarrow \bar{\alpha }$$\end{document}αj→α¯, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]\leqslant G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]=\int _\Omega |u_{\bar{\alpha }}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Gαj[uαj]⩽Gαj[uα¯]=∫Ω|uα¯-uη|2dx=Gα¯[uα¯]. Property (5.34) is once again obtained arguing by the fundamental theorem of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence, as in (iii).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }_0=\bar{\alpha }_1=+\infty $$\end{document}α¯0=α¯1=+∞, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}$$\end{document}uα¯ is affine. Thus, for every sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}} \subset (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞)2 satisfying \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j\rightarrow \bar{\alpha }$$\end{document}αj→α¯, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\alpha _j}]\leqslant G_{\alpha _j}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]=\int _\Omega |u_{\bar{\alpha }}-u_\eta |^2\;\text {d}x=G_{\bar{\alpha }}[u_{\bar{\alpha }}]. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Gαj[uαj]⩽Gαj[uα¯]=∫Ω|uα¯-uη|2dx=Gα¯[uα¯]. Property (5.34) is once again obtained arguing by the fundamental theorem of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence, as in (iii).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

In view of the lemmas above, we obtain the following characterization of the lower semicontinuous envelope of J.

Lemma 5.18

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}Ω⊂R2 be a bounded, Lipschitz domain, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J:(0,+\infty )^2\rightarrow [0,+\infty )$$\end{document}J:(0,+∞)2→[0,+∞) be the function defined in (5.28). Corollary 5.16.

Proof

We first note that the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{J} $$\end{document}J^ in (5.35) is lower-semicontinuous on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,+\infty ]^2$$\end{document}[0,+∞]2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{J} \leqslant J$$\end{document}J^⩽J in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}(0,+∞)2. Next, we denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\widetilde{J}}$$\end{document}J~ the function on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[0,+\infty ]^2$$\end{document}[0,+∞]2 defined by the right-hand side of (5.36), and observe that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} {\widetilde{J}} (\bar{\alpha }) = \Vert u_{\bar{\alpha }} - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}, \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}J~(α¯)=‖uα¯-uc‖L2(Ω)2,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\bar{\alpha }}:= {\text {argmin}}_{u\in L^1(\Omega )} G_{\bar{\alpha }}(u)$$\end{document}uα¯:=argminu∈L1(Ω)Gα¯(u) is given by (5.33). We want to show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\widehat{J} \equiv {\widetilde{J}}$$\end{document}J^≡J~. By Lemma 5.17, for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }\in [0,+\infty ]^2$$\end{document}α¯∈[0,+∞]2 there exists a sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞) such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j \rightarrow \bar{\alpha }$$\end{document}αj→α¯ and for which we have5.37\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} {\widetilde{J}} (\bar{\alpha })&=\Vert u_{\bar{\alpha }} - For this, we distinguish several cases as in the proofs of Lemma 5.17: (i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞) be any sequence such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j \rightarrow \alpha $$\end{document}αj→α. As argued before, we observe that the uniform bounds in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω) proved in Lemma 5.15 assert that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(G_{\alpha _j})_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Gαj)j∈N is an equi-coercive sequence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}L1(Ω). Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} {\widetilde{J}}(\alpha )&=\Vert u_{\alpha } - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} \leqslant \liminf _{j\rightarrow \infty } \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}\\ &= \liminf _{j\rightarrow \infty } J(\alpha _j). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}J~(α)=‖uα-uc‖L2(Ω)2⩽lim infj→∞‖uαj-uc‖L2(Ω)2=lim infj→∞J(αj). \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} )^2$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞)2 such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} })$$\end{document}J^(α¯)=J~(α¯).(iii)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} )$$\end{document}α¯1=α1∈(0,+∞), the thesis follows by observing that the same argument as in (iii) of Lemma 5.17 still holds for any sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _0^j,\alpha _1^j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(α0j,α1j)j∈N with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} (i)If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\alpha }=\alpha \in (0,+\infty )^2$$\end{document}α¯=α∈(0,+∞)2, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\alpha _j)_{j\in \mathbb {N}}\subset (0,+\infty )$$\end{document}(αj)j∈N⊂(0,+∞) be any sequence such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha _j \rightarrow \alpha $$\end{document}αj→α. As argued before, we observe that the uniform bounds in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω) proved in Lemma 5.15 assert that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(G_{\alpha _j})_{j\in \mathbb {N}}$$\end{document}(Gαj)j∈N is an equi-coercive sequence in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}L1(Ω). \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence on the convergence of minimizing sequences and energies (see [27, Corollary 7.20 and Theorem 7.8]), together with the uniqueness of minimizers of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_{\alpha _j}$$\end{document}Gαj and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G_\alpha $$\end{document}Gα, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j} \rightharpoonup u_\alpha $$\end{document}uαj⇀uα weakly-\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BV(\Omega )$$\end{document}BV(Ω) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lim _{j\rightarrow \infty } G_{\alpha _j} [u_{\alpha _j}] = G_{\alpha } [u_{\alpha }]$$\end{document}limj→∞Gαj[uαj]=Gα[uα]. In particular, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\alpha _j} \rightharpoonup u_{\alpha }$$\end{document}uαj⇀uα weakly in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}L2(Ω). Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} {\widetilde{J}}(\alpha )&=\Vert u_{\alpha } - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )} \leqslant \liminf _{j\rightarrow \infty } \Vert u_{\alpha _j} - u_c\Vert ^2_{L^2(\Omega )}\\ &= \liminf _{j\rightarrow \infty } J(\alpha _j). \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}J~(α)=‖uα-uc‖L2(Ω)2⩽lim infj→∞‖uαj-uc‖L2(Ω)2=lim infj→∞J(αj).
